title: "Humanitarian aid during the Gaza war"
---

# Humanitarian aid during the Gaza war

For humanitarian aid entered since the imposition of the Israeli blockade in 2007, see [Humanitarian aid to Gaza](77142854.md).



Workers unloading [Iranian](20826914.md) aid shipment to Gaza, late October 2023

During the [Gaza war](74998519.md), humanitarian aid entered into the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) via air, land and sea. Early in the war, significant issues arose with [humanitarian aid](434679.md). Israel's initial [blockade on Gaza](75013719.md), immediately following the [7 October attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7_October_attacks), prevented the entry of humanitarian aid for several weeks. As the war progressed, aid was allowed at limited quantities. Entities such as [Oxfam](19698439.md), the [European Union](9317.md), [United Kingdom](31717.md), and [United Nations](31769.md) stated that Israel is deliberately [blocking humanitarian aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blocking_of_humanitarian_aid).[^cite_note-1] These limitations have contributed to a severe [humanitarian crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_humanitarian_crisis_\(2023%E2%80%93present\)) and a risk of [famine](75828824.md). [Israeli airstrikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_airstrikes) and continued restrictions on aid entry led to widespread shortages of food and supplies. Distribution of aid within Gaza has also been an issue. Thousands of truckloads of aid piled up as armed men stop convoys, threaten drivers, and rifle through the cargo. Lawlessness was reported by PBS to be a major obstacle to aid distribution to southern and central Gaza. [Gaza's police](34888928.md) have refused to protect aid convoy after airstrikes killed eight police officers in [Rafah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive).[^cite_note-2]

Humanitarian aid agencies warned of the dire humanitarian consequences of aid restrictions, particularly after major Western donors announced they would cease funding [UNRWA](32270.md), the major aid relief agency in Gaza, and Israel passed legislation to [ban UNRWA from working in or with the State of Israel](32270.md#Israeli_ban_and_ICJ_case).[^cite_note-3]

In January 2024, [UN Secretary-General](162415.md) [António Guterres](471806.md) appointed then-[First Deputy Prime Minister of the Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deputy_Prime_Minister_of_the_Netherlands) [Sigrid Kaag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigrid_Kaag) as Senior Humanitarian and Reconstruction Coordinator for Gaza. By May 2024, the UN [World Food Programme](33582.md) warned that humanitarian operations were "near collapse".[^cite_note-4] In June 2024, the EU Commissioner for Crisis Management, [Janez Lenarcic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janez_Lenarcic), stated the majority of people in Gaza were "fully dependent" on humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-5] In October 2024, the United Nations found that Israel had arbitrarily blocked 83% of aid entering Gaza in the prior year.[^cite_note-6] According to [UNICEF](19698110.md), more aid workers had been killed in Gaza by summer 2024 than in any war since the founding of the United Nations, with at least 278 killed.[^cite_note-7][^cite_note-8]

Israeli imposed closure on the movement of goods and people to and from Gaza dates back to 1991. This policy was initially temporary, but eventually developed into a permanent administrative measure in March 1993. Since then, the closure has varied in intensity, but has never been completely lifted. In 1994, Israel built the [Gaza–Israel barrier](478337.md) as a security measure.[^cite_note-Sara_M._Roy-9] Since then, there are four border crossings between Israel and the Gaza Strip through the barrier: the [Kerem Shalom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerem_Shalom_border_crossing), [Karni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karni_crossing), [Erez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erez_crossing), and [Sufa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufa,_Israel#Border_crossing) crossings. All goods bound for Gaza as well as exports passing through Israel must use one of these crossings, and undergo security inspection before being permitted to enter or leave Gaza. After the [2005 withdraw of Israeli settlers from Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_Gaza), all trade was ceased and the entrance of goods was limited to a "humanitarian minimum", allowing only those goods which are "essential to the survival of the civilian population".[^cite_note-10] According to Palestinian analyst Elhasan Bakr, the prewar amount of aid trucks into Gaza was 500-600 a day, with estimates of 1,000-1,500 trucks needed to alleviate starvation.[^cite_note-11]

### 2023 October block on aid

Main article: [2023 Israeli blockade of the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israeli_blockade_of_the_Gaza_Strip)

> The amount of aid coming in is a drop in the bucket of Gaza's humanitarian needs. Before this conflict, about 100 trucks of aid alone entered Gaza \[a day]. So imagine now, with all the hostilities going on, if only this number is coming in.

— Mey al Sayegh, ([Red Cross](9720597.md))[^cite_note-12]

On 9 October, Israel implemented a [complete blockade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2023_Israeli_blockade_of_the_Gaza_Strip) on Gaza, preventing the entry of any humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-13] Egypt closed its border to prevent civilians fleeing, but said that it would allow aid to be delivered through the [Rafah crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_crossing).[^cite_note-14] A week later, despite international calls for deliveries, hundreds of tons of aid were stuck on Egypt's side of the border, as Israel bombarded the crossing amid fears of weapons deliveries, and declined to assure Egyptian authorities it would pause airstrikes for civilian aid convoys.[^cite_note-15][^cite_note-16] In Israel, aid to Gaza was reportedly prevented by far-right politicians allied with Netanyahu.[^cite_note-17] On 17 October, the UNRWA stated that there was currently "no water or electricity in Gaza. Soon there will be no food or medicine either".[^cite_note-18][^cite_note-19]

## Blockade and resumption of deliveries after October 2023

A total [blockade of the Gaza Strip](75013719.md) was announced on 9 October 2023 by the [Defence Minister of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_Minister_of_Israel), [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant). "We are putting a complete siege on Gaza … No electricity, no food, no water, no gas – it's all closed" he announced.[^cite_note-aljazeera_2023-10-09-20][^cite_note-21]

### Deliveries to southern Gaza

On 18 October, Israel announced it would allow food, water, and medicine to be delivered to a "safe zone" in the west [Khan Younis](101330.md) in southern Gaza, distributed by the United Nations.[^cite_note-Israel_says_it_will_allow_Egypt-22][^cite_note-Joe_Biden_warns_Israel-23][^cite_note-24] Later the same day, US president [Joe Biden](145422.md) announced Egypt agreed to allow 20 trucks with aid to enter Gaza by 20 October.[^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26] More than 100 trucks of aid were waiting at the Rafah crossing to enter into Gaza.[^cite_note-27] In a statement, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) stated that without electricity or fuel, however, the provided aid would fail "meeting the needs of Gaza's population".[^cite_note-28][^cite_note-Israel_says_it_will_allow_Egypt-22] On 19 October, US Special Envoy [David M. Satterfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_M._Satterfield) stated the US wanted "sustained" aid into Gaza.[^cite_note-29] The same day, a spokesman for [Oxfam](19698439.md) stated aid distribution in Gaza would be a "big challenge", and the UN reported at least 100 trucks a day of aid were needed.[^cite_note-30][^cite_note-31] On 21 October 20 trucks of aid entered Gaza.[^cite_note-32] [António Guterres](471806.md) stated it was not enough to prevent a "humanitarian catastrophe".[^cite_note-33] [Martin Griffiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Griffiths) said the UN was working to develop an "at-scale operation".[^cite_note-34] On 22 October, following the second delivery of trucks, Biden and Netanyahu stated aid would continue to be allowed into Gaza.[^cite_note-35]

### Delivery during temporary ceasefire

The amount of aid entering Gaza increased during the [temporary November ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_war_ceasefire).[^cite_note-36] On 26 November, the largest shipment of humanitarian aid reached northern Gaza since the start of the conflict nearly two months before.[^cite_note-37] Philippe Lazzarini stated the aid entering Gaza was still inadequate.[^cite_note-38] Samer AbdelJaber, a [World Food Programme](33582.md) head, stated people were hungry and desperate.[^cite_note-39] On 28 November, the [White House](33057.md) reported that over 2,000 trucks of aid had entered Gaza since 21 October.[^cite_note-40]

## Resumption of hostilities after November 2023 ceasefire

### December 2023



Aid entering Rafah through Egypt

Following the resumption of hostilities on 1 December, aid deliveries into Gaza ceased.[^cite_note-41] The IDF informed the [Palestinian Red Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Red_Crescent) that the entry of trucks was "prohibited, starting from today" until further notice.[^cite_note-42] Later the same day, the United States announced they had requested a reversal of the decision, and Israel stated it was prepared to allow aid at pre-pause levels.[^cite_note-43] On 4 December, the United Nations Office for the Coordination of Humanitarian Affairs (OCHA) stated that approximately 100 humanitarian aid trucks and 69,000 litres of fuel entered Gaza on 3 and 4 December. This was "well below" the on average 170 trucks and 110,000 litres of fuel that were delivered daily during the temporary ceasefire.[^cite_note-44] On 4 December, Lynn Hastings, a UN humanitarian coordinator, stated, "The conditions required to deliver aid to the people of Gaza do not exist" and warned of a "hellish scenario" in which aid delivery was entirely impossible.[^cite_note-45][^cite_note-47] [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell) shared a warning on social media from [Martin Griffiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Griffiths) stating an immediate ceasefire was needed for the UN to continue humanitarian operations.[^cite_note-48] WHO stated Israel shot at its humanitarian relief trucks in [Gaza City](55387.md).[^cite_note-49]

On 15 December, Israel approved the reopening of the [Kerem Shalom crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerem_Shalom_border_crossing) and announced that the US would be paying to upgrade the [Rafah crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_crossing).[^cite_note-50] Following a tour of the Rafah crossing, MEP [Barry Andrews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Andrews_\(politician\)) stated he believed Israel was deliberately delaying aid deliveries to the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-51] On 19 December, UNRWA headquarters was bombed.[^cite_note-52] UN chief Antonio Guterres stated the way Israel was conducting its offensive was creating obstacles for delivery.[^cite_note-53] On 29 December, Israel fired on a humanitarian aid truck marked with U.N. insignia.[^cite_note-54] Israel announced on 31 December it was prepared to allow aid ships from [Cyprus](5593.md) to enter Gaza.[^cite_note-55]

### January 2024

France and Jordan airdropped aid on 5 January.[^cite_note-56] On 11 January, Samer AbdelJaber, a [World Food Programme](33582.md) director, stated the organization had delivered "crucial food assistance to thousands of people facing catastrophic hunger" in [Gaza City](55387.md) for the first time in weeks.[^cite_note-57]

On 13 January 2024, UNOCHA reported the amount of aid Israel was allowing into Gaza had significantly decreased since the prior month.[^cite_note-58] Omar Shakir, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md)'s Israel-Palestine director, stated, "This is a deliberate Israeli government policy. Aid is not reaching north Gaza."[^cite_note-59] US senators [Chris Van Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen) and [Jeff Merkley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Merkley) criticized Israel's inspection of humanitarian aid as "arbitrary" and "broken".[^cite_note-60] A joint statement by the heads of [UNICEF](19698110.md), [WHO](33583.md), and the World Food Programme stated the limited quantity of aid arriving in Gaza was unable to prevent the "deadly combination of hunger, malnutrition, and disease".[^cite_note-61] A viral video showed huge crowds of hungry people rushing toward a rumored relief truck.[^cite_note-62] On 16 January, a deal was reached between Israel and Hamas to bring more aid into Gaza.[^cite_note-63] On 19 January, UNOCHA reported that nearly 70% of its aid deliveries to northern Gaza had been denied by Israel.[^cite_note-64] On 29 January, the United Nations reported that Israel had denied 29 aid missions to northern Gaza.[^cite_note-65]

In late January, at least 15 countries announced they were [suspending funding for UNRWA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNRWA_October_7_controversy).[^cite_note-66] Philippe Lazarrini, the agency chief, stated, "Our humanitarian operation, on which 2 million people depend as a lifeline in Gaza, is collapsing."[^cite_note-67] The regional director of [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) stated, "If you stop these trucks, people will die of hunger and very quickly".[^cite_note-68] UNRWA warned that without continued funding, it would be forced to cease operations by the end of February 2024.[^cite_note-69]

### February 2024

See also: [Flour Massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flour_Massacre)



Israelis blocking humanitarian aid from entering Gaza, February 2024

On 11 February 2024, the UNRWA head said a month's worth of food supply was being blocked at Israel's [Ashdod port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Ashdod).[^cite_note-70] UNOCHA reported that only six of the 24 planned United Nations aid missions to northern Gaza had been completed in the month of February.[^cite_note-71]

The World Food Programme announced on 20 February they were ceasing aid deliveries to northern Gaza, stating, "Gaza is hanging by a thread and WFP must be enabled to reverse the path towards famine for thousands of desperately hungry people".[^cite_note-72] WFP stated their last aid mission had been surrounded by "crowds of hungry people".[^cite_note-73] On 20 February, at least one Palestinian civilian was killed while waiting to receive humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-74]

In a 21 February article CNN reported that according to documentation examined by both the UN and CNN, a UN humanitarian convoy carrying food supplies was fired upon by the IDF before being blocked from entering northern Gaza on 5 February. The convoy's path had been agreed upon by the IDF and the UN, and the convoy had been stopped at an IDF holding point for over an hour when it was fired upon, causing much of its contents which included wheat flour to be destroyed. The UN blamed "Israeli naval gunfire", while CNN identified three Israeli missile ships nearby that could have shot at the convoy.[^cite_note-75]

Britain and Jordan air dropped medicine and other aid to Tal al-Hawa Hospital in northern Gaza.[^cite_note-76] Humanitarian organizations warned that a [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive) could lead to the end of even limited aid entering Gaza.[^cite_note-77] On 22 February, UNRWA chief Philippe Lazzarini stated the organization had reached its "breaking point", with continued humanitarian services now "seriously threatened".[^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79] Satellite imagery showed more than 1,000 aid trucks on the Egyptian side of the border waiting to cross into Gaza.[^cite_note-80] By late-February, only four trucks were entering per day, compared to 133 at the start of the month.[^cite_note-81] On 23 February, UNRWA said it was no longer operational in northern Gaza, where civil order had collapsed due to Israel's bombardment and restriction on food.[^cite_note-82] UNRWA stated aid hadn't reached northern Gaza in a month.[^cite_note-83]

On 25 February, satellite imagery showed as many as 2,000 humanitarian aid trucks at the Egyptian border waiting to enter Gaza.[^cite_note-84] The World Food Programme's director for emergencies stated there was enough food waiting to enter Gaza to feed the entire population, yet it was delayed due to Israel's ongoing attacks and "delays at the checkpoints".[^cite_note-85] On 26 February, [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USAID) director [Samantha Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samantha_Power) stated only 85 humanitarian aid trucks had entered in the past week, while 500 trucks were needed daily.[^cite_note-86] The same day, ten trucks were allowed to enter northern Gaza, described as a "trickle" of what was needed.[^cite_note-87] Ten people were killed waiting for aid.[^cite_note-88] People seeking aid were attacked by Israeli forces on multiple occasions.[^cite_note-89][^cite_note-90] The [Jordanian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordanian_Air_Force) conducted its largest airdrop of the war, parachuting aid to 11 sites along the Gaza coast.[^cite_note-91][^cite_note-93] The following day, the [Egyptian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_Air_Force) conducted its first aid drop of the war, dropping 45 tonnes of aid supplies.[^cite_note-94] The World Food Programme described airdrops as an option of last resort.[^cite_note-95]

On 27 February, [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) stated, "The provision of aid within the enclave is nearly impossible due to Israel's complete disregard for the protection and safety of medical and humanitarian missions and their staff, cutting people off from lifesaving aid. This reality is making the humanitarian response in Gaza a mere illusion."[^cite_note-96] The existing system of humanitarian aid was described as "broken", due to the severe restrictions on aid and ongoing Israeli bombardments.[^cite_note-97]

### March 2024

[Hanke Bruins Slot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanke_Bruins_Slot), the Dutch foreign minister, offered supply scanners to speed up the inspection of humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-98] The World Food Programme reported, "We need entry points to northern Gaza that will allow us to deliver enough food for half a million people in desperate need".[^cite_note-99] A report by [Refugees International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_International) found that Egypt had intensified its oversight and regulation of humanitarian aid trucks in response to Israeli pressures to allow in refugees.[^cite_note-100] The [Gaza Interior Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interior_Minister_of_the_Gaza_Strip) organized an armed "People's Protection Force" to replace the Gaza police that disbanded after targeted Israeli strikes and to combat [black market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_market) prices.[^cite_note-101] On 12 March, Morocco delivered 40 tons of aid from [Ben Gurion airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Gurion_airport) to the [Karem Abu Salem border crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karem_Abu_Salem_border_crossing).[^cite_note-102] The same day, the World Food Programme stated they had delivered food to northern Gaza for the first time in nearly a month.[^cite_note-103] Mohamed Shusha, the northern Sinai regional governor, stated some 7,000 trucks were waiting to enter Gaza.[^cite_note-104]

On 8 March, *[New Scientist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Scientist)* reported that aid groups lacked long-term plans to address health needs in Gaza.[^cite_note-105] [Save the Children](436885.md) described issues with aid delivery, stating, "There's so much rubble and destruction around that it is physically even hard... to deliver our supplies when they make it in".[^cite_note-106] James Elder, a [UNICEF](19698110.md) representative, stated on 17 March that it was an "outrage" that so many humanitarian trucks were waiting to enter Gaza while people starved.[^cite_note-107] On 19 March, the UN reported that less than half of its planned aid missions to northern Gaza had been allowed by Israel.[^cite_note-108] In late-March, the EU's top diplomat [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell) called on Israel to allow UNRWA head [Philippe Lazzarini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippe_Lazzarini) to enter Gaza.[^cite_note-109] On 22 March 2024, only 35 humanitarian aid trucks were allowed to enter into the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-110] On 25 March, Israel stated it would no longer work with UNRWA in Gaza.[^cite_note-111]

During a raid on [Al-Shifa Hospital](21218880.md), Israeli forces killed the security coordinator who had succeeded in the first delivery of trucks in northern Gaza in the two days prior. The killing "shattered the sense of safety" for other Palestinians working on coordinating humanitarian aid to northern Gaza.[^cite_note-112] According to Israel, the coordinator was "coordinating military activities".[^cite_note-113] Following the granting of an emergency request from South Africa, the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) ordered Israel to provide more humanitarian aid into Gaza.[^cite_note-114]

#### Airdrops



Image of US airdrop of aid into Gaza. The drop was described as "ineffective" by Oxfam, and "symbolic" by a former director of [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USAID).

On 1 March 2024, the United States began airdrops of aid into Gaza, with a White House spokesperson stating it was exploring the option of a maritime corridor to begin humanitarian aid deliveries.[^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116] Rik Peeperkorn, a WHO representative, stated, "The simplest, safest way and most effective way to deliver aid to people is through crossings. Not just Rafah. It should \[also] be Kerem Shalom."[^cite_note-117] Jeremy Konyndyk, the president of [Refugees International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_International), stated of the US airdrops: "This is not enough to make a meaningful dent in the humanitarian crisis... the Israeli military offensive has made it virtually impossible for normal humanitarian operations to exist in Gaza".[^cite_note-118]

Dave Harden, a former [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USAID) director, stated, "The airdrops are symbolic and designed in ways to appease the domestic base".[^cite_note-119] The head of [Medical Aid for Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_Aid_for_Palestinians) criticized the airdrop campaign, stating, "The US, the UK and others should ensure that Israel immediately opens all crossings into Gaza for aid".[^cite_note-120] [Oxfam](19698439.md) stated it did not support the "ineffective" aid drops and called them a way "to relieve the guilty consciences of senior US officials".[^cite_note-121] [Al Jazeera English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazeera_English) described the airdrops as an "absurd spectacle aimed more at the news cameras than the people who need it."[^cite_note-122]

On 5 March, the Israeli army prevented 14 World Food Program aid trucks from entering Northern Gaza.[^cite_note-123] In the aftermath of the [Flour massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flour_massacre), however, the UN suggested that "Israel saw quite clearly how difficult it is to deliver assistance" and that it had "much more cooperation from Israel as a result of that realisation".[^cite_note-124]

Civilians reported some of the airdropped meals were inedible without being microwaved, which was impossible since Gaza hasn't had electricity since the start of the war.[^cite_note-125] Following an airdrop that killed five people, the Gaza Media Office stated, "Dropping aid in this way is flashy propaganda rather than a humanitarian service. We previously warned it poses a threat to the lives of citizens in the Gaza Strip, and this is what happened today when the parcels fell on the citizens' heads."[^cite_note-126]

#### Temporary port

Main article: [Gaza floating pier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_floating_pier)



Construction of the floating pier, 26 April 2024

On 7 March it was announced that the process of creating a port in Gaza to receive and assist in the disbursement of aid by the United States was being finalized.[^cite_note-127] The port would reportedly take a number of weeks to set up, but would be able to receive large ships that would reportedly carry food, water, medicine and temporary shelters after the ships had been inspected by Israeli officials in Cyprus.[^cite_note-128] [David Cameron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Cameron) suggested the pier could take months to set up entirely.[^cite_note-129] The US later suggested the pier would take up to two months to build.[^cite_note-130]

The [European Commission](9974.md) and its allies — including Germany, Greece, Italy, the Netherlands, the Republic of Cyprus, the United Arab Emirates, the United Kingdom and the United States — announced their intentions to open a "maritime corridor to deliver much-needed additional amounts of humanitarian assistance by sea".[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132] In a pilot program, the [Open Arms salvage vessel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Arms_\(watercraft\)) was sent to a small port in Gaza, with aid delivered by the [World Central Kitchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen).[^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134][^cite_note-135] The US Secretary of State stated the maritime corridor was part of its plan to "flood the zone" with aid.[^cite_note-136] On 15 March, the IDF stated that 12 trucks of humanitarian aid were distributed in northern Gaza.[^cite_note-137]

A [Save the Children](436885.md) director released a statement criticizing the timeline, stating, "Children in Gaza cannot wait to eat. They are already dying from malnutrition and saving their lives is a matter of hours or days – not weeks".[^cite_note-138] The [International Rescue Committee](196452.md) also criticized the pier and called on the US to push Israel to lifts its siege and open the [Al-Muntar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karni_crossing) and [Beit Hanoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erez_Crossing) land crossings.[^cite_note-139] In a statement, [Medical Aid for Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_Aid_for_Palestinians) said, "Airdrops, temporary seaports and the like are not realistic or lasting solutions to stave off looming famine and sustain life in Gaza".[^cite_note-140] [Avril Benoit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avril_Benoit), the executive director of [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md), stated the pier was a "glaring distraction from the real problem: Israel's indiscriminate and disproportionate military campaign and punishing siege".[^cite_note-141] [Sigrid Kaag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigrid_Kaag) and the director of the [UN Office for Project Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Office_for_Project_Services) stated, "For aid delivery at scale, there is no meaningful substitute to the many land routes and entry points from Israel into Gaza."[^cite_note-142]

In April, as US troops began construction of the port, a mortar attack was struck near where it would connect with Gaza. No group claimed responsibility, but Hamas had previously stated they will "resist any foreign military presence involved with the port project".[^cite_note-143]

### April 2024

In early April 2024, following the [World Central Kitchen drone strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen_drone_strikes),[^cite_note-144] the Israeli war cabinet announced that they would allow more aid into Gaza, including the opening of the Erez border crossing.[^cite_note-145] Israel was expected to allow 300-350 aid trucks into Gaza daily, though this is still less than the pre-war amount of 500–600.[^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147] In a report published by the IDF in April 2024, it was reported that Israel had allowed the entry of 19,776 trucks of humanitarian aid into Gaza through the [Kerem Shalom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerem_Shalom_border_crossing) and [Nitsana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nitzana_Border_Crossing) border crossings. The trucks reportedly brought in 369,990 tons of aid, coupled with reportedly at least 50 air drops of about 3,000 packages and six field hospitals created.[^cite_note-148]

On 10 April, Israel claimed to allow 419 trucks into Gaza, the highest number so far. It also claimed 468 trucks going in the next day.[^cite_note-149] This was disputed by the UN, UNRWA, UNOCHA, and Red Crescent. UNOCHA said that when COGAT screens trucks, they are typically half-full and get filled on the other side. It alleges that Israel inflates the number of aid trucks through this technicality.[^cite_note-150] On the same day, Israeli defense minister Yoav Gallant announced the opening of a new crossing to allow aid at [As-Siafa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/As-Siafa).[^cite_note-151]

The *Financial Times*, meanwhile, reported that "just a trickle of humanitarian aid can enter or be distributed in Gaza each day".[^cite_note-152] On 16 April, a senior [UNOCHA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNOCHA) official stated that in the prior week, "41% of humanitarian requests to the north were denied".[^cite_note-153] The UN stated that since the beginning of April, an average of 181 trucks had entered Gaza each day.[^cite_note-154] The Canadian [Minister of International Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_International_Development_\(Canada\)) requested information from the Israeli government after the International Development and Relief Foundation stated its water truck had been destroyed in a "targeted" attack.[^cite_note-155] The United Nations stated that only 55% of its aid missions to northern Gaza in April were facilitated by Israel.[^cite_note-156]

UNOCHA stated, "Routes that remain available for use by humanitarian organizations are frequently blocked by debris, heavily congested, and contaminated with unexploded ordnance".[^cite_note-157]

### May 2024

Further information: [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive)

The Erez crossing was re-opened on 1 May. Israel said the target number of aid trucks daily was 500.[^cite_note-158] The U.S. alleged that Hamas briefly seized the first aid shipment that came through.[^cite_note-159] Israeli settlers attacked a Jordanian aid shipment,[^cite_note-160] leading [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell) to state, "It is despicable that people who lack nothing stop food reaching those in need."[^cite_note-161] The U.S. stated it was nearing completion of its temporary floating pier.[^cite_note-162]

After a Hamas rocket attack killed four Israeli soldiers and injured others at Kerem Shalom, Israel closed the border crossing. It was "reopened" after a call between Biden and Netanyahu shortly after, amidst the beginning of an Israeli incursion in Rafah.[^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164] According to the UN, however, no aid entered the crossing because there was nobody on the Palestinian side able to reach it.[^cite_note-165]

The Rafah border crossing was closed after Israel seized it.[^cite_note-166] In response, [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) stated, "This will have a devastating impact as assistance coming through this crossing is a lifeline for the whole Gaza Strip."[^cite_note-167] The spokesperson for the UN humanitarian office stated, "The two main arteries for getting aid into Gaza are currently choked off".[^cite_note-168] The UN stated it did not have access to the Rafah crossing, as it had been denied by [COGAT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coordinator_of_Government_Activities_in_the_Territories).[^cite_note-169] The Gaza Government Media Office stated on 9 May that 400 aid trucks had been denied entry into Rafah in the past 48 hours.[^cite_note-170]

International groups stated that the Rafah offensive threatened to collapse humanitarian operations.[^cite_note-171] The local partner of [ActionAid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ActionAid) stated it was pausing humanitarian operations in Rafah.[^cite_note-172] The UN ceased food distribution in Rafah, stating, "Without access across the borders to any supplies and without access to our distribution centres, we are simply unable to distribute food."[^cite_note-173][^cite_note-174] UNOCHA stated, "There's almost nothing left to distribute in Gaza".[^cite_note-175] The [Independent Commission for Aid Impact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_Commission_for_Aid_Impact) stated Israel was blocking UK aid from entering Gaza.[^cite_note-176] [Gisha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gisha_\(human_rights_organization\)) stated COGAT was dumping goods on the Israeli side of the [Kerem Shalom crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerem_Shalom_crossing).[^cite_note-177] On 23 May, UNOCHA stated, "If aid does not begin to enter Gaza in massive quantities, desperation and hunger will spread".[^cite_note-178] Food waiting to enter into Rafah had rotted by late-May.[^cite_note-179]

The [Gaza floating pier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_floating_pier) was damaged in a storm and taken to Israel for repairs.[^cite_note-180] Doctors Without Borders stated they were seeing a "further strangulation of the Gaza Strip" as fuel shortages had resulted the amount of water being distributed reduced by seven-eighths.[^cite_note-181] The UN stated aid deliveries dropped 67% in May.[^cite_note-182] On 29 May, World Central Kitchen announced it was pausing its operations in Rafah amidst Israeli attacks.[^cite_note-183]

### June 2024

On 8 June, the United States announced deliveries would begin again following repairs made to the pier.[^cite_note-184] On 10 June, the World Food Programme stated it was pausing use of the temporary pier following the [Nuseirat refugee camp massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuseirat_refugee_camp_massacre) in order to assess the security situation.[^cite_note-185] The same day, the U.S. resumed airdrops.[^cite_note-186] UNICEF stated that one of its humanitarian aid convoy was denied entry on 14 June to northern Gaza.[^cite_note-187] On 15 June, the United States stated it was removing the pier due to inclement weather.[^cite_note-188] COGAT stated 8,600 aid and commercial trucks had entered Gaza since 2 May; however, much of it was piled up at crossings because of the danger of transporting it.[^cite_note-189] A UN spokesperson stated, "The lack of any police or rule of law in the area makes it very dangerous to move goods there".[^cite_note-190] [Humanity & Inclusion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanity_&_Inclusion) stated Israeli forces bulldozed its aid warehouse, stating they were "extremely concerned that attacks against humanitarian facilities and operations are becoming a pattern".[^cite_note-191] Jeremy Konyndyk, the president of [Refugees International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_International), stated it was "almost impossible" to find warehouses in Rafah.[^cite_note-192] On 25 June, senior UN officials stated they would cease humanitarian operations in Gaza if Israel did not improve safety for aid operations.[^cite_note-193]

The U.S. government stated that much of the aid brought through its temporary pier was piling up on the beach, as aid groups were unable to distribute it.[^cite_note-194] The UN secretary-general stated aid delivery was made more difficult by a lack of security protecting trucks, stating, "Israel does not even allow the so-called blue police to escort our convoys because it's local police linked to the local administration".[^cite_note-195]

### July 2024–December 2024

The Israeli government and [Starlink](45111627.md) were reportedly in talks to set up a communications system over the Gaza Strip to improve security for UN humanitarian aid efforts.[^cite_note-196] The United Nations stated that an Israeli evacuation order affecting one-third of the Strip had "wiped out" efforts to improve humanitarian aid deliveries.[^cite_note-197] The UN said that they would be bringing in more personal safety equipment and armored vehicles starting 16 July following approval from Israeli officials.[^cite_note-198] Humanitarian aid deliveries piled up for long stretches at a time due to distribution challenges posed to humanitarian aid organizations within Gaza.[^cite_note-199] The World Food Programme stated that Israel's military actions in Gaza City had created an "unpredictable and volatile situation" and were limiting its operations there.[^cite_note-200] Samantha Power, the head of USAID, stated she had received assurances from Israel that humanitarian aid workers would be allowed to work more safely.[^cite_note-201] The United States permanently closed its temporary aid pier.[^cite_note-202] [ActionAid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ActionAid) stated 20 of its aid trucks had been stuck in Egypt for at least two months.[^cite_note-203]

A study for the period of January to July 2024 found that daily available food energy supplied to Gaza was 3004 kcal per person.[^cite_note-204]

In mid-August 2024, Corinne Fleischer, the World Food Programme's Middle East regional director, stated it was "very difficult" to bring food into Gaza.[^cite_note-205] Peace activists from the [Freedom Flotilla Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_Flotilla_Coalition) stated they would attempt to break Israel's blockade on Gaza and deliver humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-206] The [Government of Japan](30858300.md) committed US\$2.5 million to the World Food Programme to provide emergency food assistance.[^cite_note-207]

The United Nations stated it was pausing its Gaza operations in late-August 2024.[^cite_note-208] A [UNOCHA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNOCHA) official stated, "Major humanitarian operations have never stopped fully, but they are at the most curtailed they have ever been due to inaccessibility."[^cite_note-209] The World Food Programme similarly announced it was pausing humanitarian movements in the Gaza Strip, after an Israeli attack on a WFP team near an Israeli check point.[^cite_note-210] By October 2024, private traders were responsible for 60% of aid flow into the Gaza Strip, according to Israeli military data.[^cite_note-211] The same month, UNRWA reported that intensified military operations in the northern Gaza Strip were forcing them to shut down services there.[^cite_note-212] James Elder, the spokesperson for [UNICEF](19698110.md), stated, "We see now what is probably the worst restrictions we've seen on humanitarian aid, ever".[^cite_note-213] By the end of October 2024, humanitarian aid to the Gaza Strip reached its lowest levels since the conflict began.[^cite_note-214]

[Mordechai Kahana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mordechai_Kahana), the Israeli-American CEO of Global Delivery Company, stated that he was in talks with the Israeli government for a \$200 million plan to provide security and humanitarian aid to enclosed areas for Palestinians.[^cite_note-215] According to Kahana, his message to Palestinian residents was that "you don't want to mess with us" and that "terrorists will get a bullet".[^cite_note-216] In October 2024, the Israeli Knesset passed a bill [banning UNRWA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_ban_on_UNRWA?action=edit\&redlink=1), which [Amnesty International](18947898.md) warned amounted to the criminalization of humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-217] The head of UNRWA stated that an average of only 30 trucks entered the Gaza Strip per day in the month of October 2024.[^cite_note-218]

### 2025–present

In early May 2025, it was announced that the United States would proceed with a new system for providing humanitarian aid via private companies with Israeli military guarding distribution centers due to allegations of Hamas stealing aid. The aid will be distributed under the aegis of the [Gaza Humanitarian Foundation](79923808.md). The director of the [UN's office for the Coordination of Humanitarian Affairs (OCHA)](1602284.md) Jens Laerke announced the UN's lack of participation as the system is not in line with the organizations values. Others in the UN have called the announced system a weaponization of humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-219]

On August 18, a potential deal was brokered by Egypt and Qatar. Hamas agreed to release half the hostages in two batches for a 60-day ceasefire, although the Israeli government states it's only interested in the release of all hostages both living and dead in exchange for the end of the war.[^cite_note-BBC18August2025-220]

On August 22, 2025, the Integrated Food Security Phase Classification, or IPC, has declared the most severe Phase 5 for some parts of Gaza including Gaza City. Between mid-August and September 30, 2025, an additional 640,000 persons are expected to face Phase 5 conditions. A statement from the Israel Prime Minister's office called this IPC declaration a "modern blood libel." The Prime Minister's statement went on to say, "Israel, will continue to act responsibly, ensuring aid reaches Gaza's civilians while destroying Hamas's terror machine."[^cite_note-ABCNews22August2025-221]

During the war, there were some reports of women being sexually exploited by some aid workers or local men in exchange for aid, food, money or the promise of jobs. Aid organizations and experts said exploitation commonly occurs during times of conflict and desperation. Other reports of abuse and exploitation have come to light during crises such as in South Sudan and Congo.[^cite_note-222]

On 4 December 2025, [Xi Jinping](2017814.md), the leader of China, announced at a press conference that the country will provide \$100 million in aid to the Palestinians to help ease the humanitarian crisis in Gaza and support reconstruction efforts.[^cite_note-reuters251204-223]

In April 2026, the [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](8980088.md) in [Afghanistan](737.md) announced that 530 tonnes of humanitarian aid, valued nearly \$500,000, would be delivered to Gaza.[^cite_note-224]
