## Issues with delivery

On 27 October 2023, Lynn Hastings, the UN's humanitarian coordinator for Palestine, stated Israel opposed the delivery of humanitarian aid to northern Gaza.[^cite_note-Israel_'clearly'_opposes_aid-228] As a result, UN staff would need to risk their own lives if it was determined such aid would be "lifesaving" to people in need.[^cite_note-Israel_'clearly'_opposes_aid-228] [Philippe Lazzarini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippe_Lazzarini) stated "soon many more will die" from Israel's blockade.[^cite_note-229]

Direct attacks on telecommunications infrastructure by Israel, electricity blockades and fuel shortages have caused the near-total collapse of Gaza's largest cell network providers.[^cite_note-elpais20240129-230][^cite_note-theguardian-231] The blackouts have also impeded emergency services, making it more difficult to locate and access the time-critical injured,[^cite_note-elpais20240129-230] and have impeded humanitarian aid agencies and journalists as well.[^cite_note-elpais20240129-230] The United Nations announced a communications blackout had brought aid delivery to a "complete halt."[^cite_note-232] A [humanitarian zone](35072890.md) was announced in the Khan Younis area on 29 October 2023, along with a claim that aid trucks would increase "significantly."[^cite_note-233] On 30 October 2023, [OCHA](1602284.md) director Lisa Doughten pressured the UN Security Council for the use of extra entry points to Gaza, suggesting the [Kerem Shalom border crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerem_Shalom_border_crossing) as the only entry equipped for rapidly processing a sufficiently large number of trucks.[^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235] The United Nations announced it no longer had enough fuel to deliver humanitarian aid in Gaza on 13 November 2023, leading Canadian Foreign Minister [Melanie Joly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melanie_Joly) to say, "This is not acceptable."[^cite_note-236] The UN suspended aid delivery again on 17 November 2023 due to the shortage of fuel and the cutoff of communications.[^cite_note-237]

In March 2024, following the passage of US legislation barring [UNRWA](32270.md) funding until at least March 2025, the head of the UN organization stated, "As the backbone of the humanitarian response, any gap in funding to UNRWA will compromise access to food, shelter, primary health care & education".[^cite_note-238] The UN and other aid agencies stated visa delays were part of restrictions on humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-239] In May 2024, [Forensic Architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forensic_Architecture) stated Israel had attacked humanitarian aid on at least 80 occasions.[^cite_note-240] In June 2024, the United Nations stated the lack of safe conditions were further hindering aid operations, stating, "As the occupying power, it is incumbent upon the Israeli authorities to restore public order and safety as far as possible and facilitate safe humanitarian access".[^cite_note-241]

In late-August 2024, a United Nations official reported they were pausing nearly all aid operations in Gaza.[^cite_note-242] The World Food Programme also temporarily suspended movements in Gaza after coming under fire from soldiers at a nearby Israeli check point.[^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244]

### Israeli blocking of aid



Trucks with [humanitarian aid](434679.md) waiting to cross from Egypt into the Gaza Strip

Before being allowed entry into Gaza, humanitarian aid trucks are put through stringent inspections by Israel. Janti Soeripto, president and chief executive of Save the Children, explained to reporters the convoluted regulations that cause items to be turned back, like [sanitary pads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menstrual_pad) due to a [nail clipper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nail_clipper) included in the hygiene kit, or sleeping bags because they had zippers. A humanitarian official in contact with the Israeli COGAT unit has raised claims that the rejections are due to the unit operating with only an obsolete list from 2008 and a lack of official guidance.[^cite_note-cnn20240301-245] Others, such as the [European Union](9317.md)'s top diplomat, [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell), have accused Israel of intentionally using starvation as a weapon of war.[^cite_note-246] Israel also does not allow humanitarian aid bound for Gaza to be purchased in Israel or the West Bank, or to use Israel ports.[^cite_note-Tanis-247] It has also closed all but one of the Israel-Gaza checkpoints.[^cite_note-Tanis-247] Egyptian Foreign Minister [Sameh Shoukry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sameh_Shoukry) stated that "Israeli obstacles" were impeding the delivery of humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-248] The [World Health Organization](33583.md) stated their "requests to deliver supplies are often blocked or refused" by Israel.[^cite_note-249] [Antonio Guterres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Guterres), the UN secretary-general, stated that alleviating conditions in Gaza "requires Israel removing the remaining obstacles and chokepoints to relief".[^cite_note-250] A September 2024 investigation found that [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Agency_for_International_Development) and other agencies found Israel that deliberately [blocked humanitarian aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blocking_of_humanitarian_aid), but the U.S. Secretary of State rejected their findings.[^cite_note-251]

An investigation found that Israel was blocking cancer medications, sleeping bags, drinking water purification tablets, and maternity kits from entering Gaza, leading the [Save the Children](436885.md) US president to state she had "never seen anything like the level of barriers being put in place to hamper humanitarian assistance".[^cite_note-cnn20240301-245] MP [Rosena Allin-Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosena_Allin-Khan) stated that Israel had prevented water filters from entering Gaza.[^cite_note-252] The UN reported Israel had rejected aid trucks for carrying medical scissors.[^cite_note-253] On 6 March, Israel continued to block a major US shipment of flour from entering Gaza, after having already blocked it from entering for 46 days.[^cite_note-254] On 7 March 2024, Israeli police blocked an aid convoy organized by the Jewish-Arab cooperation group [Standing Together](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standing_Together_\(movement\)).[^cite_note-255]

The president of the [American Near East Refugee Aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Near_East_Refugee_Aid) stated, "Some governments—not just the US but others as well—who thought that they would make more headway with the Israelis on land aid threw up their hands and said, 'Well, let's do airdrops.'"[^cite_note-256] In an interview with the *New Yorker*, US Senator [Chris Van Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen) stated, "Administration officials have recognized that the Netanyahu government has put up unacceptable barriers to the delivery of humanitarian assistance into Gaza".[^cite_note-257] [Catherine Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine_M._Russell), the chair of [UNICEF](19698110.md) described Israel's bureaucratic approval system as [Kafkaesque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kafkaesque).[^cite_note-258] In March, UNRWA said that Israel was no longer allowing the agency to deliver aid to northern Gaza.[^cite_note-259] [Martin Griffiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Griffiths) stated, "The decision to block its food convoys to the north only pushes thousands closer to famine. It must be revoked."[^cite_note-260] The EU's Josep Borrell stated, "Enough food for the people starving in Gaza is stockpiled and waiting to enter through land routes".[^cite_note-261]

[Gisha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gisha_\(human_rights_organization\)), an Israeli human rights organization, stated Israel was blocking [dual-use items](1003982.md) and that it had petitioned the Israeli Supreme Court to order Israel to meets its obligations to civilians in Gaza.[^cite_note-262] In April 2024, the [Freedom Flotilla Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_Flotilla_Coalition) stated [a scheduled aid trip to Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Gaza_Freedom_Flotilla) had been delayed by an administrative roadblock "initiated by Israel in an attempt to prevent our departure."[^cite_note-263] A [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Agency_for_International_Development) internal assessment reportedly found Israel "does not currently demonstrate necessary compliance" with a requirement that countries not impede "the transport of delivery of United States humanitarian assistance".[^cite_note-264] In July 2024, 13 human rights organizations, including Oxfam, Doctors Without Borders, Save the Children and the Norwegian Refugee Council, stated Israel was blocking humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-265] UNICEF spokesperson James Elder stated that Israel was intentionally restricting aid from entering Gaza.[^cite_note-266]

In September 2024, the [Norwegian Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Refugee_Council) reported that 83% of required food aid was failing to enter the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-267] In October 2024, humanitarian aid deliveries dropped to their lowest levels in seven months, according to individuals involved with aid.[^cite_note-268] After the United States told Israel it could face consequences unless it increased aid to northern Gaza, Israeli officials stated they would hold an "emergency meeting" regarding humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-269] Soon after, the Israeli Knesset [passed a bill barring contact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_ban_on_UNRWA?action=edit\&redlink=1) between [UNRWA](32270.md) and Israeli officials within three months, which is necessary for UNRWA to coordinate transferring aid into the Gaza Strip, due to Israel controlling all crossings.[^cite_note-270] The United States responded by saying they had not seen enough progress by Israel to increase humanitarian aid deliveries.[^cite_note-271] Several days later, the U.S. stated it would "follow the law" if Israel did not comply with its recommendation to allow in humanitarian aid by its deadline.[^cite_note-272]

In November 2024, it was reported that Israel allowed limited delivery of aid to [Beit Hanoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hanoun), in northern Gaza, but before the aid arrived, the Israeli army forced inhabitants to evacuate the area or risk being harmed in airstrikes.[^cite_note-273]

In February 2025, according to the Israeli Defense Ministry, a truck carrying aid through the Erez corridor was searched and found to be smuggling hundreds of smartphones, and other electronics. The driver was subsequently arrested.[^cite_note-274]

#### Suspension and ban of aid organisations

In December 2025, Israel announced the suspension of permits for 37 non-governmental and aid organisations, including [Action Against Hunger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Action_Against_Hunger), [Médecins Sans Frontières](20498.md), [Mercy Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercy_Corps), [Norwegian Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Refugee_Council), and [Relief International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relief_International) from operating in Gaza for allegedly failing to meet [new requirements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guidelines_for_the_Registration_of_Organizations) to hand over personal details of Palestinian and international staff working in the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-275][^cite_note-276] On 1 January 2026, Israel announced the enforcement of the ban and required the organisations to cease their activities by 1 March.[^cite_note-277]

In response, the United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights [Volker Türk](71721726.md) said the suspension is "outrageous", stating that "such arbitrary suspensions make an already intolerable situation even worse for the people of Gaza" and urged Israel to reverse the decision.[^cite_note-278] United Nations secretary-general [António Guterres](471806.md) also urged Israel to reverse the ban and stated that the "suspension risks undermining the fragile progress made during the ceasefire".[^cite_note-279] In Israel, 17 human rights and advocacy organisations condemned the ban, stating it "undermines principled humanitarian action, endangers staff and communities, and compromises effective aid delivery".[^cite_note-280] Other countries including Canada, France, Finland, Japan, Norway, Switzerland, and the United Kingdom also issued condemnations against Israel's decision.[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282] The [European Union](9317.md) warned that Israel's suspension of aid groups would hinder "life-saving" assistance from reaching the population of Gaza.[^cite_note-283]

#### Israeli protests against aid

See also: [Israeli blockade of aid delivery to the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_blockade_of_aid_delivery_to_the_Gaza_Strip) and [Gaza war protests in Israel § Protest against humanitarian aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_in_Israel#Protest_against_humanitarian_aid)

On 21 December 2023, Israeli activists attempted to block the [Kerem Shalom crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerem_Shalom_crossing) to prevent humanitarian aid from entering the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-284] On 9 January, captives' families were stopped by police trying to block humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-285] On 19 January, families of captives promised "extreme actions", including blocking humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-286] Hundreds protested against the entry of humanitarian aid at the Kerem Shalom crossing on 24 January 2024.[^cite_note-287] Protesters again blocked aid on 25 and 26 January.[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290] On 28 January, the IDF declared the Karem Abu Salem crossing a "closed military area".[^cite_note-291] Four settlers were arrested for tear-gassing and throwing rocks at aid delivery truck drivers.[^cite_note-292] Protesters again blocked aid on 30 January.[^cite_note-293] On 30 January, [Benny Gantz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benny_Gantz) and [Gadi Eisenkot](5249926.md) stated they were examining limiting humanitarian aid into Gaza.[^cite_note-294] On 31 January, National Security minister [Itamar Ben-Gvir](54504115.md) called on Netanyahu to cease sending aid to Gaza.[^cite_note-295]

30 were arrested protesting against aid for Gaza on 31 January.[^cite_note-296] On 1 February, protests blocked aid trucks from leaving the [Port of Ashdod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Ashdod).[^cite_note-297] On 2 February, protesters blocked the [Nitzana Border Crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nitzana_Border_Crossing).[^cite_note-298] On 6 February, [Channel 12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_12_\(Israel\)) reported that 132 aid trucks had been prevented from entering the Karem Abu Salem crossing.[^cite_note-299] On 7 February, Israelis set up tents at the Karem Abu Salem to block aid from entering Gaza.[^cite_note-300] Protesters blocked aid at the Nitzana border crossing on 9 February.[^cite_note-301] Protesters blocked the Karem Abu Salem crossing on 12 February.[^cite_note-302] On 14 February, an individual blocking humanitarian aid from entering Gaza stated, "We cannot give them good foods".[^cite_note-303] UNOCHA reported that only 20 trucks of aid entered Gaza on 17 February.[^cite_note-304] Aid was again blocked on 19 February.[^cite_note-305] By 19 February, humanitarian aid entering Gaza had experienced a "clear decline" since the start of the month.[^cite_note-306]

In early May 2024, Israeli settlers attacked a humanitarian aid shipment traveling to the Erez crossing, according to the Jordanian Foreign Ministry.[^cite_note-307]

Then, Israeli activist group Tzav 9 claimed responsibility for the stoppage of an aid convoy at [Tarqumiyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarqumiyah) in [Hebron Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebron_Hills) on 13 May 2024, vowing: "No aid goes through until the last of the hostages returns." Tzav 9 further provided a quote from an Israeli that stated: "Blocking the trucks is a noble and understandable act for anyone with a sound mind." Some of the aid was spilled during the stoppage by right-wing protesters, leading to police arresting four people, but after the police left, two aid trucks were set ablaze.[^cite_note-308] The aid delivery workers said that the aid convoy's escort of Israeli soldiers did nothing to prevent the attack by Israeli settlers, with one aid worker stating that the convoy travelled on a "special army road that civilians could not cross", but was attacked by "at least 400 settlers", who were "throwing stones" and dumping aid, while the Israeli "army did not provide us with any kind of protection", despite being "present and watching what was happening. The army was at the service of the settlers."[^cite_note-309]

BBC News reported in late May 2024: "Right-wing activists, including Jewish settlers living in the occupied West Bank, have uploaded dozens of videos of crowds, including some very young children, hurling food onto the ground and stamping on boxes of aid" meant for Gaza. Other videos, BBC News describes, "show Israeli vigilantes stopping lorries in Jerusalem and demanding that drivers show papers proving they are not transporting aid to Gaza. Their faces are uncovered and they appear to be acting with complete impunity." Peace activists, both Jews and Arab, have mobilised in an attempt to protect the aid; these peace activists have claimed that text messages exist showing aid-attacking activists asking for and gaining help from the Israeli police and the Israeli army.[^cite_note-310] In June 2024, the [Palestine Red Crescent Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Red_Crescent_Society) stated Israel was blocking humanitarian aid from entering through the [Rafah crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_crossing), in violation of ICJ orders.[^cite_note-311]

### Allegations against Hamas

When asked about Hamas's responsibility for the safety of civilians, Hamas leader [Mousa Abu Marzook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mousa_Abu_Marzook) allegedly replied that "it is the responsibility of the [United Nations](31769.md) to protect them... \[and] it is the responsibility of the occupation to provide them with... services".[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313] On 28 October, *The New York Times* reported that Hamas had stockpiled food, water, medicine and sanitary products in underground caches, in amounts that would allow it to continue fighting for several months without resupply.[^cite_note-314] On 12 November, *[Kan 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kan_11)* aired a video taken by a Gazan civilian, that appeared to show Hamas policemen beating civilians approaching a truck carrying humanitarian aid for food, before allegedly taking the supplies for themselves.[^cite_note-315] According to *[The Algemeiner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Algemeiner)*, an unnamed "[Fatah](11104.md) TV anchor" claimed that Hamas killed humanitarian aid workers to "prevent any activity by any \[other] party" and "ensure Hamas control over the aid".[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317] Israel has also claimed Hamas has stolen aid.[^cite_note-318][^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320][^cite_note-321] However, US officials said they do not have evidence to support Israel's claims.[^cite_note-DeYoung_Hudson_2024_g056-322][^cite_note-323] On 13 September Israeli Channel 12 news played alleged intercepted recordings purporting to show Hamas operatives discussing how to disperse overflowing Gaza aid that they had seized. In the recordings of radio conversations, an operative says "We have trucks overflowing with goods." The second operative declines the offer, saying "we have everything. In the meantime, we have no room in the stores." He then suggests they send the goods to Khan Younis.[^cite_note-324]

In an initiative aiming at shifting control of Gaza's population away from Hamas, U.S. aid foundation, [Gaza Humanitarian Foundation](79923808.md) outlined a \$2 billion plan to establish "Humanitarian Transit Areas" in and potentially outside Gaza. These camps are described as large-scale, voluntary facilities where Palestinians could temporarily reside, undergo deradicalization, reintegrate, and prepare for potential relocation. Palestinian officials, including Hamas, have strongly rejected the proposal. A spokesperson for the Hamas-run Gaza media office dismissed GHF as a front for Israeli intelligence operating under a false humanitarian pretext.[^cite_note-325]

In July 2026, UN Deputy Special Coordinator Ramiz Alakbarov issued an official UN statement, according to which UN aid distributions to Gaza were suspended after [Hamas](13913.md) militants entered a food distribution site in [Jabalia](453723.md) and a [World Food Programme](33582.md) warehouse and assaulted two truck drivers. Alakbarov added that "These incidents are not isolated. They are completely unacceptable and reflect an ⁠increasingly dangerous pattern of intimidation, violence and obstruction, including smuggling attempts, targeting and abusing humanitarian operations." Hamas denied the allegations.[^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327]

### Allegations against the UN

In March 2024 Ynet reported that the UN was blaming Israel and feeding into the Hamas narrative, while distributing of aid is hampered by inability of organizations, and by Hamas taking over the aid.[^cite_note-328]

In October 2024 COGAT officials said data presented by the UN is collected through flawed methods, leading to accusations of dishonesty and delays in aid delivery.[^cite_note-329] In November 2024, the head of COGAT Major General Ghassan Alian, posted a video showing 600 trucks worth of humanitarian aid in Gaza that the UN did not distribute to Gazans.[^cite_note-330]

In June 2025, the chair of the GHF Rev. Dr. Johnnie Moore, accused the UN for "playing politics with the lives of Gazans," because the UN refused to cooperate with GHF in Gaza.[^cite_note-331]

In August 2025, [ILTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ILTV) reported from Gaza's side of the Kerem Shalom crossing that "thousands and thousands of tons of humanitarian aid \[...] is sitting here in the boiling sun". [Former Israeli Ambassador to the US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ambassadors_of_Israel_to_the_United_States) [Michael Oren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Oren) said that the "UN refuse\[s] to distribute it to the people of Gaza," saying that the "UN is in cahoots with Hamas".[^cite_note-332]

During a diplomatic meeting in Estonia, Israeli president [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) stated that "The UN is holding hundreds of trucks, almost 800 trucks. The UN can distribute and is failing to distribute."[^cite_note-333]

## Casualties

### Killing of aid workers

See also: [Killing of health workers in the Gaza war § UN workers](75657910.md#UN_workers), [World Central Kitchen drone strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen_drone_strikes), and [Attacks on humanitarian workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attacks_on_humanitarian_workers)

From 7 October 2023 to 23 June 2024, 193 [United Nations](31769.md) relief workers were killed in the Gaza Strip, making it the deadliest conflict for UN workers in world history.[^cite_note-334] According to Jeremy Konyndyk, the president of [Refugees International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_International), "The U.S. concern about these casualties remains almost purely rhetorical. There is no policy leverage being put behind it whatsoever. Beyond expressing concern and expressing regret, that's where it stops."[^cite_note-335] By March 2024, at least 165 United Nations staff had been killed in Gaza since 7 October and more than 150 facilities attacked.[^cite_note-336] The United Nations stated that 2023 was the deadliest year on record for aid workers, with Gaza representing more than half of all killed around the world.[^cite_note-337]

[David M. Satterfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_M._Satterfield) criticized Israel killing police guarding truck convoys, stating it had made the safe distribution of aid "virtually impossible".[^cite_note-338] A [Norwegian Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Refugee_Council) representative stated on 20 February 2024 that Israel's targeting of aid convoys and police was "unacceptable".[^cite_note-339] Following the death of one of their aid workers — the fifth American aid worker killed in Gaza — [Anera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Near_East_Refugee_Aid) released a statement: "We demand an independent investigation into his death, which threatens our team's ability to function safely and deliver aid to civilians facing starvation".[^cite_note-340]

In March 2024, Israel bombed a United Nations food distribution center, killing one UN staff member and wounding 22 others.[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-343] It was one of the UN's last remaining distribution centers, leading UN humanitarian aid chief [Martin Griffiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Griffiths) to state the UN's aid teams "must be protected".[^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345] [Stephane Dujarric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephane_Dujarric), the UN Secretary-General's spokesman, stated, "It's another tragic symbol of the conditions under which our humanitarian colleagues work every day in Gaza."[^cite_note-346] The next day, Israel reportedly killed 8 people in an attack on an aid warehouse in [Nuseirat refugee camp](18168164.md).[^cite_note-347] On 15 March 2024, Gaza government officials stated that 56 people had been killed in Israeli attacks on aid distribution centers in just the prior 48 hours.[^cite_note-348] On 18 March 2024, the Gaza Media Office stated Israel assassinated the official in charge of coordinating aid to northern Gaza.[^cite_note-349]

On 19 March 2024, at least 23 people were killed when Israeli fighter jets targeted a group of humanitarian aid coordinators at the Kuwaiti roundabout.[^cite_note-350] Earlier the same day, an Israeli airstrike killed the police director in charge of facilitating humanitarian aid trucks in northern Gaza.[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352] The Gaza Media Office stated on 20 March that more than 100 aid workers had been killed in the prior seven days.[^cite_note-353] The UN Secretary-General [Antonio Guterres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Guterres) stated, "Some of them, with decades of experience, said they have never encountered a situation as difficult as this. Aid workers need resources, access and safety – now."[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355] On 29 March 2024, an Israeli attack reportedly killed a Palestinian police force that helped bring aid to northern Gaza.[^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357] The UN criticized the attack, stating, "Such killings must stop. Effective aid delivery to those most in need requires civil order".[^cite_note-358]

On 2 April 2024, the IDF bombed [World Central Kitchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen) staff. The [drone strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen_drone_strikes) killed seven humanitarian aid workers.[^cite_note-359] According to UN Secretary-General [Antonio Guterres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Guterres), the strikes brought the total number of aid workers killed since 7 October to 196 people.[^cite_note-360] The president of Refugees International called the killings "part of a clear pattern" and a war crime.[^cite_note-361] [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) stated the killings were an example of how "international humanitarian law is not respected".[^cite_note-362] The [Polish Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Foreign_Ministry) stated, "Poland objects to the disregard for international humanitarian law".[^cite_note-363] [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) secretary-general Christopher Lockyear stated, "This pattern of attacks is either intentional or indicative of reckless incompetence."[^cite_note-364] In May 2024, the first international United Nations aid worker was killed while in a UN marked vehicle in Rafah.[^cite_note-365] [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) stated that Israel had repeatedly targeted the known locations of humanitarian aid workers.[^cite_note-366]

In June 2024, UN spokesperson [Stéphane Dujarric](10302459.md) stated, "Humanitarian operations have repeatedly been in the crosshairs in Gaza. The risks, frankly, are becoming increasingly intolerable."[^cite_note-367] In July 2024, the bodies of three Palestinians working to secure aid at the Kerem Shalom crossing were found, with their hands reportedly bound.[^cite_note-368] The same month, an Israeli air attacked killed at least four of the [Al-Khair Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Khair_Foundation)'s aid workers.[^cite_note-369] An Israeli strike killed at least four Anera aid workers in late-August 2024.[^cite_note-370] An Israeli strike killed a member of [Mercy Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercy_Corps) in October 2024.[^cite_note-371] In April 2025, it was reported that two workers from the [Hafezzi Charitable Society of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hafezzi_Charitable_Society_of_Bangladesh?action=edit\&redlink=1) were killed by Israeli forces.[^cite_note-372]

### Killing of aid seekers

See also: [Flour massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flour_massacre), [March 2024 Kuwait Roundabout attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2024_Kuwait_Roundabout_attacks), and [Rafah aid distribution killings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_aid_distribution_killings)

This section is an excerpt from [Gaza Strip famine § Israeli attacks on aid distribution](75828824.md#Israeli_attacks_on_aid_distribution).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Gaza_Strip_famine\&action=edit)]

According to [Forensic Architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forensic_Architecture), a research group at [Goldsmiths, University of London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goldsmiths,_University_of_London), more than 40 Israeli attacks on humanitarian aid seekers have been documented. According to the group, these were not isolated events, but were rather "systematic in nature".[^cite_note-373]

[Human Rights Watch](46530.md) reported eight incidents since the start of the war until May 2024 in which aid workers were attacked by Israeli forces despite providing locations for Israeli leaders. The eight incidents, it said, reveal serious flaws in the so-called "conflict prevention mechanism" – a mechanism designed to protect aid workers and allow them to safely deliver life-saving humanitarian aid to the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_HSU200-374]

On 25 January 2024, the Gaza Health Ministry reported an Israeli attack on aid seekers had killed 20 and wounded 150.[^cite_note-375] Israel bombed a truck loaded with food head toward northern Gaza on 5 February.[^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377] On 6 February, Israeli forces reportedly opened fire on people waiting for food aid trucks in Gaza City.[^cite_note-378] UNOCHA stated it was the fifth report of Israeli firing upon people waiting for humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-379] On 18 February, multiple instances of Israeli sniper attacks on civilians seeking humanitarian assistance were reported.[^cite_note-380] On 20 February, at least one Palestinian civilian was killed while waiting to receive humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-381] People seeking aid were attacked by Israeli forces on multiple occasions.[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383] UNOCHA stated on 27 February that "aid convoys have come under fire and are systematically denied access to people in need".[^cite_note-384] On 28 February, medical sources in Gaza City reported three people were killed while waiting for aid on [al-Rashid Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Rashid_Street_\(Gaza\)).[^cite_note-385]

On 29 February, more than 100 people seeking humanitarian died in the [Flour Massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flour_Massacre) by Israeli gunfire at the Al Nabulsi roundabout west of Gaza City.[^cite_note-386] The United Nations called for an investigation on 1 March into the killing of humanitarian aid seekers, stating it had "recorded at least 14 incidents involving shooting and shelling of people gathered to receive desperately needed supplies".[^cite_note-387]

On 2 March, three people in [Beit Hanoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hanoun) were killed while picking herbs for food.[^cite_note-388] On 3 March, at least nine people were killed while waiting for humanitarian aid in an Israeli airstrike in Deir el-Balah.[^cite_note-389][^cite_note-390] Later on the same day, dozens of civilians were killed in an Israeli attack on aid seekers at the Kuwaiti roundabout in Gaza City.[^cite_note-391] The Ministry of Health called it a "horrific massacre".[^cite_note-392] On 4 March, another attack was reported at the Kuwaiti roundabout, after thousands of people waited all day for humanitarian aid, Israeli soldiers opened fire on them as soon as the trucks arrived.[^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_Another_Israeli_attack-393] Al Jazeera stated the attacks on aid seekers had become "a near-daily occurrence".[^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_Another_Israeli_attack-393] On 6 March, eight people were wounded after Israel fired live rounds at people seeking humanitarian aid at the Nabulsi roundabout.[^cite_note-394] On 7 March, five people were killed while waiting for aid at the Nabulsi roundabout.[^cite_note-395] On 8 March, several people seeking humanitarian aid were reportedly killed by Israeli open fire at the Kuwait Roundabout.[^cite_note-396] By 12 March, Israel had killed an estimated 400 humanitarian aid seekers in Gaza.[^cite_note-397] Israeli attacks on humanitarian aid seekers was described as the "new normal" for Palestinians in northern Gaza.[^cite_note-398]

On 13 March 2024, at least nine aid seekers were shot and wounded by Israeli troops at the Kuwaiti roundabout.[^cite_note-399] On 14 March 2024, Israeli troops [fired on Palestinians receiving aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Roundabout_mass_killings) at the Kuwaiti roundabout, killing 21 and injuring more than 150 others.[^cite_note-400][^cite_note-401] In a statement on 15 March, the UN humanitarian aid chief Martin Griffiths stated that attacks on aid seekers "cannot be allowed to continue".[^cite_note-402] The [Norwegian Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Refugee_Council) stated the attacks "shouldn't be happening".[^cite_note-403] On 19 March, at least 23 people were killed when Israeli fighter jets targeted a group of aid coordinators at the Kuwaiti roundabout.[^cite_note-404]

On 1 April 2024, an [Israeli drone fired three consecutive missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen_aid_convoy_attack) at three cars belonging to the [World Central Kitchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen) (WCK), killing seven aid workers who had been distributing food in the northern Gaza Strip, which has been pushed close to famine by [Israel's siege](75208470.md) and blockade during the Israel–Hamas war.[^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_ap1-405] In June 2024, eight people were reportedly killed at an UNRWA aid distribution training center near Gaza City.[^cite_note-406]

From May 2025[^cite_note-407] [GHF](79923808.md) was set up as the sole aid distributor in Gaza but repeated and regular mass shootings and killings at the distribution sites means that they have been described as "slaughter masquerading as aid".[^cite_note-408]

Survivors of the repeated mass killings perpetrated at the distribution sites have called this Israeli-backed operation "traps" rather than aid.[^cite_note-409][^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_theguardian20250623-410] [The Center for Constitutional Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Constitutional_Rights) and 14 other human rights organizations have warned that the GHF could be liable for [complicity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complicity_in_genocide) in [war crimes](48626.md), [crimes against humanity](61099.md), and [genocide against Palestinians](75668427.md).[^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_theguardian20250623-410]

Members of the IDF and of the Israeli police have been providing information to far right Israeli settlers about upcoming aid truck convoys delivering food to Gaza so they could intercept the deliveries to vandalize and destroy them. A spokesperson for the radical right Israeli group [Tzav 9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tzav_9) stated that the group has been blocking aid trucks to Gaza since January 2024, acknowledged receiving advance information from Israeli forces but denied being the ones who burned trucks. Videos taken in May 2024 show Israelis intercepting aid trucks and destroying food aid. Many Israeli and Palestinian witnesses reported that the IDF soldiers sent to accompany and protect the aid trucks watched as the settlers attacked the convoys but did nothing to protect them.[^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412]

On 8 March, five were killed when they were struck by airdropped aid after its parachute failed to open.[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-414] A witness stated, "The parachute didn't open and fell down like a rocket on the roof of one of the houses".[^cite_note-415] [Stephane Dujarric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephane_Dujarric) called it a "tragic accident".[^cite_note-416] On 9 March, a second airdrop wounded aid seekers who were taken to [al-Shifa Hospital](21218880.md).[^cite_note-417]

It was reported that on 14 March by Gaza's health ministry that at least 20 people were killed and 155 wounded by Israeli shelling when they were queued to wait for humanitarian aid to be disbursed in Kuwait Square. The IDF denied IDF forces fired upon aid seekers and that "armed Palestinians" did.[^cite_note-418] On 26 March, the Gaza government media office stated that 12 people had drowned attempting to retrieve aid parcels dropped in the sea.[^cite_note-419] Verified footage on 31 March showed a dead body entangled in an aid package parachute.[^cite_note-420] The Government Media Office stated in May 2024 that a 14-year-old boy had been seriously injured after opening "a booby-trapped can of food".[^cite_note-421]

On 1 June 2025, 32 Palestinians were killed and 200 others injured in an Israeli tank attack at a food distribution site in Rafah, located in southern Gaza. The aid was being distributed by the Gaza Humanitarian Foundation (GHF), an organization supported by Israel and the United States. The United Nations and other humanitarian organizations do not collaborate with GHF, as they believe it facilitates Israel's complete occupation of Gaza.[^cite_note-422]

In September 2025, after a year-long investigation, *[The New Humanitarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Humanitarian)* reported that from 1 January 2024 until 9 September 2025, a total of 2,957 aid seekers were killed and nearly 20,000 others were injured by Israeli forces.[^cite_note-423]

### Killing of looters

In November 2024, Hamas said it killed 20 armed young men from local [Bedouin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Bedouin) tribes who were stealing aid.[^cite_note-424]

## Reactions

### Israeli

In a January 2024 press conference, Netanyahu boasted that Israel had only provided minimal aid stating; "We provide minimal humanitarian aid,...If we want to achieve our war goals, we give the minimal aid."[^cite_note-cnn20240301-245] On 23 February it was reported that over two-thirds of Jewish Israelis opposed the movement of humanitarian aid into Gaza through a survey by the [Israeli Democracy Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Democracy_Institute). The survey also offered the option of allowing humanitarian aid into Gaza via international bodies that are proven to be unlinked to Hamas or [UNRWA](32270.md) ([after Israeli allegations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNRWA_October_7_controversy)) and a majority of Jewish Israelis still opposed humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-425]

The confusion about what humanitarian aid is being allowed into Gaza and what is being rejected, has prompted the Israeli rights group [GISHA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gisha_\(human_rights_organization\)) to file a [Freedom of Information Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_information_laws_by_country#Israel) request in February 2024. The request is for the Israeli government to release details of any new restrictions on aid since the 7 October attacks.[^cite_note-426] The [Israeli Welfare Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Labor,_Social_Affairs_and_Social_Services) paused visa renewals to humanitarian aid workers in February 2024, stating it couldn't investigate applicants' potential ties to armed groups.[^cite_note-427]

In May 2024, five Israel human rights organizations sued the government for its restrictions on humanitarian aid, stating, "It is inconceivable that the respondents, who admit to not having even the faintest idea about the extent of the aid required for residents of the Gaza Strip, are claiming that they have fulfilled their obligations – and even beyond".[^cite_note-428] [Ron Dermer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Dermer), the Israeli minister of strategic affairs, denied there was a famine, stating statements to the contrary were "just all false".[^cite_note-429] In June 2024, Israeli national security minister [Itamar Ben-Gvir](54504115.md) stated Israel should "reduce the humanitarian aid that enters the strip".[^cite_note-430]

In April 2025, Israeli Defense Minister [Israel Katz](19041378.md) expressed support for the blockade of humanitarian aid to Gaza, stating, "Israel's policy is clear: no humanitarian aid will enter Gaza, and blocking this aid is one of the main pressure levers preventing Hamas from using it as a tool with the population."[^cite_note-431]

### Palestinian

In response to news that the United States was building a temporary port to receive humanitarian aid on the Gaza coast, a displaced Palestinian told [Al Jazeera English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazeera_English): "All these American weapons are killing our kids and killing us wherever we go. We don't need aid from them. We need them to stop the killing, stop the death."[^cite_note-432] A director at the [Gaza European Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_European_Hospital) stated it was more important to focus on "lifting the siege on the Gaza Strip and opening the crossings".[^cite_note-433] In March 2024, Gaza officials criticized campaigns for humanitarian aid airdrops as "flashy propaganda rather than a humanitarian service".[^cite_note-434]

Hamas political leader [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md) said that if Israel agreed to a [ceasefire](75313349.md) in the Israel–Hamas war, if humanitarian corridors would be opened, and aid would be allowed into Gaza, Hamas would be "ready for political negotiations for a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution) with Jerusalem as the capital of Palestine."[^cite_note-435]

### Academics

[Mohamed Elmasry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Elmasry), an analyst at the [Doha Institute for Graduate Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doha_Institute_for_Graduate_Studies), stated of the US beginning airdrops: "They want to be able to be able to say: We told Israel not enough aid was going in and we even did these air drops – so we weren't complicit in this genocide".[^cite_note-436] [Rami Khouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rami_Khouri), a journalist and professor at the [American University of Beirut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_University_of_Beirut), criticized the United States' plan to build a port in Gaza, stating, "I'm just waiting for the day the Israelis come and bomb the port because they've bombed everything else that the EU and other donors have funded in the occupied territories."[^cite_note-437] Marc Owen Jones, a professor at [Hamad Bin Khalifa University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamad_Bin_Khalifa_University), stated that Biden's announcement of a pier in Gaza was "propaganda".[^cite_note-438] Saul Takahashi, a professor at [Osaka Jogakuin University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osaka_Jogakuin_University), stated if the US or EU cared about Palestinians, "They would not pretend like they are Hollywood action heroes airlifting supplies into Berlin, but they would stop the endless flow of weapons to Israel".[^cite_note-439]

Mohammed al-Masri, a researcher at the Palestinian Center for Research and Strategic Studies, asked, "Who will provide security to the humanitarian aid being sent to the port, and who will carry out the actual distribution? Who will manage this large relief operation?"[^cite_note-440] Mohammed al-Masri, a researcher at the [Arab Center for Research and Policy Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Center_for_Research_and_Policy_Studies), stated, "If the US was serious, it would have pressured Israel into opening the land crossings and allowing aid and relief in."[^cite_note-441] In late-March 2024, professor Marc Owen Jones stated, "Biden is breaking, or at least not enforcing, the [Leahy Laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leahy_Law)".[^cite_note-442]

### Journalists

Imran Khan, an [Al Jazeera English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazeera_English) journalist, wrote about the US plan for a new port: "There is a much more efficient way of doing it as it was going on for 20 years before October 7 – the land crossings into the Gaza Strip."[^cite_note-443] The editorial board of the *[Financial Times](136566.md)* wrote in early-March 2024, "More than any other issue, the lack of aid illustrates the impotence of the US and its western allies... As the occupying force, it is Israel's responsibility to ensure there is sufficient food for the hungry."[^cite_note-444] In May 2024, [Marwan Bishara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marwan_Bishara), Al Jazeera's senior political analyst, stated, "Israel is not contributing to the aid of Gaza, everything is coming from the outside world... Israel is doing nothing – zero – for the people of Gaza."[^cite_note-445]

### International

During a call with Netanyahu on 15 February 2024, UK prime minister [Rishi Sunak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rishi_Sunak) "highlighted the scale of the humanitarian crisis in Gaza and urged Israel to fully open the Kerem Shalom crossing and allow the maritime delivery of international aid through Ashdod port".[^cite_note-446] On 18 February, the UK Prime Minister and the European Commission President stated "significantly more aid" was needed in Gaza.[^cite_note-447] On 20 February, [William, Prince of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William,_Prince_of_Wales) stated, "There is a desperate need for increased humanitarian support to Gaza. It's critical that aid gets in".[^cite_note-448] During a trip by [Benny Gantz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benny_Gantz) to the UK, foreign minister [David Cameron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Cameron) stated that Israel needed to change the amount of aid entering the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-449] Cameron told reporters that to avoid famine and a continued spread of diseases at least 500 trucks a day were needed in Gaza.[^cite_note-450]

On 28 February 2024, a spokesman for Qatar's [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](35709864.md) stated, "We have not seen any real pressure from the international community to allow full and unconditional entry of aid. Aid should be freely provided without restrictions."[^cite_note-451] [King Abdullah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Abdullah_II_of_Jordan) stated that humanitarian aid to Gaza needed to be doubled.[^cite_note-452] [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) stated, "People in Gaza are starving... The Israeli government must do more to significantly increase the flow of aid. No excuses".[^cite_note-453] In a call, the US Secretary of Defense told the Israeli Defense Minister there was an "urgent need" to surge humanitarian aid throughout Gaza.[^cite_note-454] During the [2024 State of the Union Address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_State_of_the_Union_Address), the US president stated, "Humanitarian assistance cannot be a secondary consideration or a bargaining chip. Protecting and saving innocent lives has to be a priority".[^cite_note-455] On 13 March, the EU and five other countries called on Israel to open more land crossings into Gaza.[^cite_note-456]

The Norwegian Foreign Minister [Espen Barth Eide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Espen_Barth_Eide) stated, "Israel must ensure increased access of vital food supplies and aid into and within Gaza".[^cite_note-457] New Zealand Minister of Foreign Affairs [Winston Peters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Peters) called on Israel to take "all necessary and effective steps to ensure, without delay, the unhindered provision of urgently needed basic services and humanitarian assistance." [^cite_note-458] Following the [killing of World Central Kitchen workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen_drone_strikes), U.S. president Joe Biden stated, "Israel has not done enough to protect aid workers".[^cite_note-459] In a call with Netanyahu, Rishi Sunak stated, "Far too many aid workers and ordinary civilians have lost their lives in Gaza and the situation is increasingly intolerable".[^cite_note-460] U.S. Senator [Tim Kaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Kaine) stated, "The amount of aid allowed by Israel to enter Gaza has been painfully slow and dramatically inadequate".[^cite_note-461]

On 11 December 2023, 6 major humanitarian organizations presidents —including: [CARE USA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CARE_USA), [Mercy Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercy_Corps), the [Norwegian Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Refugee_Council), [Oxfam America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxfam_America), [Refugees International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_International), and [Save the Children](436885.md) — penned a joint op-ed in *[The New York Times](30680.md)* in which it says, "We have seen nothing like the siege of Gaza".[^cite_note-462] In July 2024, the [Associated Press](18935732.md) found the U.S. and Israel were allowing tax-deductible donations to Israeli far-right groups blocking humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-463]

#### Freedom House

[Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House) issued a cautionary statement, highlighting the escalating severity of the humanitarian crisis, and urged the Israeli government to adhere to international humanitarian law.[^cite_note-464]

#### EU's humanitarian aid

The EU's humanitarian aid chief [Janez Lenarcic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janez_Lenarcic) called on Israel to open its land crossings.[^cite_note-465] In a joint statement, Lenarcic and [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell) called the situation "beyond catastrophic" and called on Israel to open its land crossings in order to "secure impactful food assistance for the entire population of Gaza, the provision of specialised medical aid to address malnutrition and the implementation of public health measures".[^cite_note-466] In a statement, the [World Bank](36752.md) said, "We join the international community in calling for immediate, free, and unimpeded access of medical supplies, food and life-essential services" to Gaza."[^cite_note-467]

#### Doctors Without Borders

According to a [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) video shared by [Amnesty International](18947898.md) head [Agnès Callamard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agn%C3%A8s_Callamard): "This brutal annihilation of an entire population health system stretches beyond what humanitarian aid can fix."[^cite_note-468] On 4 December, [Red Cross](15487.md) president [Mirjana Spoljaric Egger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirjana_Spoljaric_Egger) visited the Gaza Strip, stating, "The things I saw there are beyond anything that anybody must be in a position in order to describe."[^cite_note-469]

### Humanitarian organizations

On 26 February 2024, UNRWA stated that the failure to deliver more humanitarian aid into Gaza was a "man-made disaster" caused by Israel's "security constraints and temporary closures at both crossings".[^cite_note-470] [Antonio Guterres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Guterres) stated a [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive) would be the "final nail in the coffin" of its aid programmes.[^cite_note-471] The spokesman for the [UN High Commissioner for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_High_Commissioner_for_Human_Rights), Jeremy Laurence, stated, "Border crossings must be fully opened and the necessary steps must be taken to ensure the free and safe movement of aid convoys to civilians wherever they are".[^cite_note-472] In October 2024, during an interview on *[Amanpour & Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amanpour_&_Company)*, the [UNICEF](19698110.md) spokesperson James Elder described the situation in Gaza as "everything that the international community ever feared, but worse somehow: no aid and more attacks on civilian areas".[^cite_note-473]

[Sigrid Kaag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigrid_Kaag) stated that Israel was not opening land border crossing due to "domestic sensitivities".[^cite_note-474][^cite_note-475] James McGoldrick, a top UN official, stated aid wasn't entering Gaza because "Israel decided that politically and militarily they wanted to do it, and more important, they could do it, and they've gone ahead and done it."[^cite_note-476] [Tor Wennesland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tor_Wennesland) described the opening of the international maritime corridor as a response to "deep frustration in the international community that we can't get in sufficient supplies".[^cite_note-477] Jeremy Konyndyk, the president of [Refugees International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_International), responded to international humanitarian [airdrops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airdrop), stating, "Facilitating airdrops – and driving media coverage around them – gives the public appearance that Israel is cooperating with humanitarian efforts. The fact that they need be considered is a major policy failure".[^cite_note-478] In a report, Refugees International further found that Israel had "consistently and groundlessly" blocked humanitarian aid from entering Gaza.[^cite_note-479][^cite_note-480]

The deputy director of [Humanity & Inclusion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanity_&_Inclusion) stated that he was stunned by the conditions in Gaza — the worst he had ever seen in his life — stating that he had never seen such a "[bombardment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombing_of_Gaza) of an extremely densely populated and closed-off area, and a near-complete lack of access for humanitarian aid".[^cite_note-481] In March 2024, [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) stated, "The food, water, and medical supplies so desperately needed by people in Gaza are sitting just across the border. Israel needs to facilitate rather than block the flow of supplies."[^cite_note-482] A group of Israel's 12 most prominent human rights organizations stated Israel was failing to comply with the [International Court of Justice](14918.md)'s [interim ruling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_v._Israel_\(Genocide_Convention\)#Ruling_on_provisional_measures) to facilitate humanitarian aid into Gaza.[^cite_note-483]

[Agnès Callamard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agn%C3%A8s_Callamard), the head of [Amnesty International](18947898.md), stated, "Airdrops, the construction of a port are a sign of powerlessness and weakness on the part of the international community".[^cite_note-484] A group of 25 humanitarian organizations issued a joint statement, saying, "States cannot hide behind airdrops and efforts to open a maritime corridor to create the illusion that they are doing enough to support the needs in Gaza. Their primary responsibility is to prevent atrocity crimes from unfolding".[^cite_note-485] A Refugees International report found that the Israeli blocking of aid was creating "apocalyptic" famine-like conditions.[^cite_note-486] The president of the [International Rescue Committee](196452.md) stated airdrops were "unsafe, ineffective and dehumanizing".[^cite_note-487]

In May 2024, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) stated Israel was in defiance of the ICJ's measure to facilitate humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-488] In late-August 2024, an Israeli airstrike killed four humanitarian aid workers in the Gaza Strip, leading World Food Programme director [Cindy McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cindy_McCain) to state, "This is totally unacceptable and must change immediately. We have repeatedly asked for a functioning deconfliction system in Gaza, and yet the current arrangements have failed."[^cite_note-489]
